Handoff: notes/w50-stepoff-fall.md | fp.ts — airY / groundY, the step-off fall | **The user: *"when i jump off of stuff i teleport straight down. please fix this."* A regression from the collider-height work — before it nothing could be stood on, so nothing could be stepped off.** **CAUSE LOCATED, VERIFY IT BEFORE BUILDING ON IT:** `fp.ts:553` is `this.airY = Math.max(0, this.airY + this.vy * dt)`, and `airY` is **height above the ground**, with world Y computed as `groundY(x, z) + airY`. Standing on the pickups cab roof, `groundY` returns **1.415** (via `standTop`) and `airY` is **0**. Walk off the edge and `groundY` returns the street — **0.000** — in that same frame, so world Y goes 1.415 to 0.000 instantly. **There is no fall to have: the player is not falling, the floor moved and took him with it.** The fix is that leaving a surface must convert the height you had into a real fall — keep world Y and let gravity bring it down — rather than re-basing to the new ground. **A SECOND EFFECT, IN THE SAME LINES, WORTH CHECKING WHILE YOU ARE THERE:** `:549` gates jumping on `airY === 0 && vy === 0`, which is **true the instant you step off** a raised surface, so stepping off a car hands you a fresh jump in mid-air. He has not reported it, but it is the same root. **`fp.ts` IS THE HIGHEST-RISK FILE IN THE PROJECT AND THE 2 m SIDEWALK LANE IS SACRED.** Stage it, commit each stage, and prove the ordinary world is unchanged with `fp before`/`after`/`fpdiff`. **Check what else reads `airY` or assumes `ground + airY`** before changing its meaning — the kerb, the stairs and the storey picker all deal in ground heights. **Other builders may hold `fp.ts`** (items 85 and 98 are both against `pickSpot`) — run `./scripts/claim.sh --stale` and coordinate or wait. DONE WHEN: stepping off the pickup bed, its cab roof and the sedans boot lid all produce a real fall at the same gravity as a jump; you cannot jump again in mid-air; walking off a kerb still feels as it does now; and you have WALKED all of it rather than warped. |

The lock is already held at that moment, so the copy is atomic and costs nothing. **Do NOT re-track QUEUE.md in git to solve this** — that reintroduces the exact reverting bug the untracking fixed, and it is documented. **Do NOT make the snapshot depend on a builder remembering to call it.** DONE WHEN: killing the file mid-session and restoring from the newest snapshot loses at most one queue operation, the history directory is gitignored, and a snapshot is provably written by all three scripts. |

| 210 | DONE seventyone — 210 DONE, but THE ROW'S PREMISE IS FALSE IN EVERY CLAUSE and the desk should read this before the LEDGER moves. The row says 'a diffuser marked selfLit is being DIMMED at night... find why the grader is reaching this material despite the flag... the flag may be on the mesh, or the material may be SHARED'. Measured: (1) it is NOT a diffuser -- it is the daylight slot WINDOW in the back wall of every cell, int-jail.ts:748, a 0.04x0.44x0.80 box behind four bars; (2) the grader is NOT reaching it -- props.ts:977 is 'if (Math.abs(wp.x) > 100) return; // interiors keep their own light' and this room is at x~1006, which is why the probe has printed userData.graded=FALSE on it every run since sixtyfour wrote it; (3) it is NOT shared -- one material, one purpose; (4) it is SUPPOSED to dim, and the line directly above it has said so since it was written: 'It DIMS WITH THE WORLD -- a bright window at two in the morning is the tell that a room is a set'. So the answer to 'was it the flag or a shared material' is NEITHER: it dims ITSELF, from its own ctx.onFrame, on purpose. AND THE ROW'S DONE WHEN WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A BUG -- 'the diffuser keeps its colour after dark' means making a jail cell window glow white at 2am, which is exactly what the author guarded against, and I did not do it. THE REAL DEFECT IS THE OPPOSITE: IT WAS NOT GETTING DARK ENOUGH. At 02:00 it read #b3b7ba -- a LIGHT GREY, the brightest thing in the cell at two in the morning. Cause: it read the frame's night, the hud's raw wash curve, whose NIGHT_STOPS (hud.ts:1225) TOP OUT AT 0.58 and at 02:00 sit flat on [0,0.58] -- it never reaches 1. Reproduces to the byte: 0.87 - 0.72*0.58 = 0.4524 linear = sRGB 0xb3, the measured value. The slot only ever travelled 58% of the way to the darkness the code already asked for. ct/int-library.ts:677-686 DOCUMENTS THIS EXACT TRAP having paid for it -- 'READ scene.userData.nightFactor, NOT the frame's night. They are two different quantities with almost the same name... I shipped the wrong one for one build' -- and that warning was written down in the neighbouring file and the jail was written against the wrong one anyway. FIX, following the library's precedent: read scene.userData.nightFactor, and lerp sRGB endpoints rather than setRGB arithmetic (the library's SECOND documented trap at :687 -- setRGB writes LINEAR, so hand-computed coefficients render brighter than they read). Endpoints are the OLD FORMULA'S OWN evaluated properly: day n=0 = #f0f3f6, BYTE-IDENTICAL to what noon already showed so daylight does not move; night n=1 = #6c6f76. Derived from the code that was there, not chosen by me. A fragment shader is INVISIBLE to anything reading `material.color` from JS** — which is exactly why three of the four dead mutation cases (item 229) died in that one commit. **(2) `scripts/grade-sane.mjs`s ceiling clause is now VACUOUS.** Its own header records **156–166 material-hours over 1.0 at the ramp hours**; today it measures **0, peak 0.0000.** It is passing because there is nothing left for it to see, for the same reason. **Either re-point it at pixels or retire the clause with a comment saying the grading moved to the GPU** — but do not leave a clause that cannot fail. **Both want population floors: measuring zero must FAIL.** DONE WHEN: `glow.mjs` is green for the right reason or red for a real one, its sampling matches where the effect now lives, `grade-sane`s ceiling clause either measures something or is honestly retired, both have floors, and you state the pixel figures you measured. |

| 239 | DONE ninetytwo — item 239: end-of-run liveness. MEASURED the real mechanism first — 99 of 141 registered checks call page.goto ONCE, so the world lives in browser memory and a dead server changes nothing: door301 killed at t+6s of a 12.9s run exited 0 saying 'the door holds'. checks.mjs only probed after FAILING checks, so a death the running check survived printed a green tick and a death during the LAST check was never probed. Added endOfRun/reportEndOfRun to lib/server-state.mjs (single source of liveness, composes probeWithRecovery, exits 3 not 1 per GOTCHAS 32); checks.mjs now probes after EVERY check (+0.16s across 143 rows, measured 1.11ms median) and reports row accounting; interiors-walk counts rooms walked vs registered. BEFORE/AFTER on the same command: exit 0 green -> exit 1 SERVER DIED. 3 negative cases watched + silent-on-healthy control; w92-endofrun-cases drives the real classifier 7/7 against real sockets incl. the 'LOST 6 rooms' message. Full suite on built bundle: 104 pass, 17 red ALL INHERITED (I-clip is item 242's origin cars; note-hashes cites others' notes), 0 spurious SERVER DIED. typecheck 0, health 0, sweep clean. | A RUN WHOSE SERVER DIES MIDWAY STILL REPORTS GREEN — the startup floor cannot catch it | **Reported by worker eightytwo, whose own server was killed mid-run — it caught that only by noticing an unrelated notification. This is the thirteenth check-lies-to-us defect this week and it undermines all the others.** Every check now carries a **startup population floor** — "if I measured nothing, FAIL" — which is the house rule that fixed a dozen vacuous passes. **But a floor at startup cannot see a server that dies at 60% through a run.** The legs already done report their results, the legs never attempted report nothing, and **the suite prints green over a world that stopped existing partway.** **We have already been bitten by the visible version of this: a containment sweep once reported "0 escapes" about a world whose server had died.** That one was caught by a dead-server guard; **this is the same failure a stage later, where the guard has already passed.** **THE FIX IS AN END-OF-RUN LIVENESS CHECK, not a bigger startup floor.** Confirm the server is still serving *after* the last leg, and confirm the number of legs that actually ran equals the number registered. **A run that completes fewer legs than it started must FAIL, naming how many were lost.** **⚠ There is a related classifier already built and it is the right precedent: `scripts/lib/server-state.mjs` distinguishes `ok` / `empty` / `dead` / `recovered`, written after `npm run build` was wrongly blamed for killing previews — it empties `dist/` for ~220 ms and a check latched on one 404, condemning a 12-minute run.** Use it rather than inventing a fourth notion of "is the server alive". **Prove it with a negative case: kill the preview mid-run and watch the suite go RED with an honest message**, not silently green. DONE WHEN: a mid-run server death fails the run, the failure names how many legs were lost, `server-state.mjs` is the single source of liveness, and the negative case is watched. |
